7.2
Defining
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}

In this section, we will define
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
, which maps the purification from querying
\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued}}
to purification of
W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}
. We know that the purification of
\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued}}
is in the good subspace, hence is in the span of
\lvert\mathfrak{G}(\overline{S})\rangle
. We start by thinking about what
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
should do for a single
\lvert\mathfrak{G}(\overline{S})\rangle
.

The intuition towards defining
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
is the following: Given any path
›\in\overline{S}
, we know that it signifies multiple interweaving queries to
V_{X}^{(i),\mathsf{mid}}
with outputs from one being partially fed as input into the next. We define
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
to convert these interweaving queries to
W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}
and
W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}
. We refer the reader to figures in the
Section

2.4
.

Again, when we have interweaving queries to
W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}
and
W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}
, we see that the output of one feeding into the exists as a maximally entangled pair between the two databases (similar to
Section

6.4
). Hence, we can sample these maximally entangled pairs and append them to a state structure parameter. Let’s denote these by
\overrightarrow{u}
and
\overrightarrow{v}
.

Formally, we define
\mathsf{comp}
as below: Given
›=({\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{L}},\overrightarrow{x^{LL}},\overrightarrow{y^{LL}},w^{LL}_{1},w^{LL}_{2})\in\overline{S}
with
\mathsf{len}(›)>2
, then define for
\overrightarrow{u^{LL}}\in\{0,1\}^{(\mathsf{len}(›)-1)n},\overrightarrow{v^{LL}}\in\{0,1\}^{(\mathsf{len}(›)-1){\lambda}}

\displaystyle\lvert\mathsf{comp}(›,\overrightarrow{u^{LL}},\overrightarrow{v^{LL}})\rangle=

\displaystyle\qquad\big|\{(x_{0}^{LL}||w^{LL}_{1}||x_{1}^{LL},u_{1}^{LL}||v_{1}^{LL}||y_{1}^{LL}),(u_{2}^{LL}||v_{2}^{LL}||x_{3}^{LL},u_{3}^{LL}||v_{3}^{LL}||y_{3}^{LL}),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\ldots,(u_{n-3}^{LL}||v_{n-3}^{LL}||x_{n-2}^{LL},u_{n-2}^{LL}||v_{n-2}^{LL}||y_{n-2}^{LL}),(u_{n-1}^{LL}||v_{n-1}^{LL}||x_{n-1}^{LL},y_{0}^{LL}||w^{LL}_{2}||y_{n-1}^{LL})\}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\otimes\lvert\{(u_{1}^{LL}||v_{1}^{LL}||x_{2}^{LL},u_{2}^{LL}||v_{2}^{LL}||y_{2}^{LL}),\ldots,(u_{n-2}^{LL}||v_{n-2}^{LL}||x_{n-1}^{LL},u_{n-1}^{LL}||v_{n-1}^{LL}||y_{n-1}^{LL})\}\rangle

On any
›=({\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{L}},\overrightarrow{x^{LL}},\overrightarrow{y^{LL}},w^{LL}_{1},w^{LL}_{2})\in\overline{S}
with
\mathsf{len}(›)=2
,
\overrightarrow{u^{LL}}=(),\overrightarrow{v^{LL}}=()
, then define

\displaystyle\lvert\mathsf{comp}(›,\overrightarrow{u^{LL}},\overrightarrow{v^{LL}})\rangle=\lvert\{(x_{0}^{LL}||w^{LL}_{1}||x_{1}^{LL},y_{0}^{LL}||w^{LL}_{2}||y_{1}^{LL})\}\rangle\lvert\emptyset\rangle

We think of
\overrightarrow{u^{LL}}
and
\overrightarrow{v^{LL}}
the simulated
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
.

Similarly, define
\mathsf{comp}
on
›
with first element
{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{R}},{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{L}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}
. Next, we define an operation that takes
\overline{S}
, a set of
\overrightarrow{u}
’s and
\overrightarrow{v}
’s, and give a combined database:

Formally, let
\overline{S}
with
a=\mathsf{count}(\overline{S})
and
b=\mathsf{len}(\overline{S})
. Let
{\mathcal{U}}=\{\overrightarrow{u^{i}}\}_{i}\in\{0,1\}^{(b-a)n}
and
{\mathcal{V}}=\{\overrightarrow{v^{i}}\}_{i}\in\{0,1\}^{(b-a){\lambda}}
, then define

\displaystyle\lvert\mathbb{F}(\overline{S},{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{V}})\rangle=

\displaystyle\lvert\bigcup_{›_{i}\in\overline{S}}\mathsf{comp}(›_{i},\overrightarrow{u^{i}},\overrightarrow{v^{i}})\rangle

Finally, we define
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\mathfrak{G}(\overline{S})\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{(b-a)n}\left((2^{{\lambda}}-a)\ldots(2^{{\lambda}}-b+1)\right)}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{U}}\in\{0,1\}^{(b-a)n}\\
{\mathcal{V}}\in(\{0,1\}^{{\lambda}}\setminus\imaginary(\overline{S}))^{b-a}_{\mathrm{dist}}\end{subarray}}\lvert\mathbb{F}(\overline{S},{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{V}})\rangle

We define the notation
\mathfrak{F}
as

\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\mathfrak{G}(\overline{S})\rangle=\lvert\mathfrak{F}(\overline{S})\rangle

Then we prove that for each query,
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
simulates the database, i.e. we prove the following:

Lemma 7.8
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

•

Forward query:

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

•

Inverse query:

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued},\dagger}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

We prove the above in
Section

7.4
but before that we will finish proving
7.4
in
Section

7.3
using
Lemma

7.8
.

7.3
Proof of
7.4
: Closeness between
\mathbf{H}_{3}
and
\mathbf{H}_{4}

Denote the initial joint state in
\mathbf{H}_{3}
by

\lvert\psi_{0}\rangle:=\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{ABC}}\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{D}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}.

For
i\in[2t]
, denote the joint state right after the
i
-th query by

\lvert\psi_{i}\rangle:=\begin{cases}W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}A_{(i+1)/2}\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle\quad&\text{, if $i\equiv 1\bmod{2}$}\\
W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}B_{i/2}\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle\quad&\text{, if $i\equiv 0\bmod{2}$}.\end{cases}

The output of
\mathbf{H}_{3}
is

\Tr_{\mathsf{ST}}\left(\lvert\psi_{2t}\rangle\!\langle\psi_{2t}\rvert\right).

Similarly, denote the initial joint state in
\mathbf{H}_{4}
by

\lvert\phi_{0}\rangle:=\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{ABC}}\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{D}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S_{1}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T_{1}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S_{2}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T_{2}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S_{3}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T_{3}}}.

For
i\in[2t]
, denote the joint state right after the
i
-th query by

\lvert\phi_{i}\rangle:=\begin{cases}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}A_{(i+1)/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle\quad&\text{, if $i\equiv 1\bmod{2}$}\\
\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}inv}}B_{i/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle\quad&\text{, if $i\equiv 0\bmod{2}$}.\end{cases}

The output of
\mathbf{H}_{4}
is

\Tr_{\mathsf{S_{1}S_{2}S_{3}T_{1}T_{2}T_{3}}}\left(\lvert\phi_{2t}\rangle\!\langle\phi_{2t}\rvert\right).

We prove the following claim by induction: for
i\in[2t]
,
\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i}\rangle-\lvert\psi_{i}\rangle\|_{2}=O(i^{3}/2^{{\lambda}})
.

•

Base case:
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{0}\rangle=\lvert\psi_{0}\rangle
.

•

Induction hypothesis: Suppose
\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle-\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle\|_{2}=O((i-1)^{3}/2^{{\lambda}})
.

Consider the following two cases:

Case 1:
i
is odd:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i}\rangle-\lvert\psi_{i}\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}A_{(i+1)/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}A_{(i+1)/2}\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle\|_{2}

(by expanding the definition of
\lvert\psi_{i}\rangle
and
\lvert\phi_{i}\rangle
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}A_{(i+1)/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}A_{(i+1)/2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\|{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}A_{(i+1)/2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}A_{(i+1)/2}\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle\|_{2}

(by the triangle inequality)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}A_{(i+1)/2}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}})A_{(i+1)/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle\|_{2}+\|W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}A_{(i+1)/2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle-\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle)\|_{2}

(since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
and
A_{(i+1)/2}
commute)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}A_{(i+1)/2}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}})\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\norm{\Ocomp\ket{\phi_{i-1}}-\ket{\psi_{i-1}}}_{2}

(by
Lemma

3.6
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(i^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})+O((i-1)^{3}/2^{{\lambda}}).

(by
Lemma

7.8
and the induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(i^{3}/2^{{\lambda}})

Case 2:
i
is even:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i}\rangle-\lvert\psi_{i}\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}inv}}B_{i/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}B_{i/2}\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle\|_{2}

(by expanding the definition of
\lvert\psi_{i}\rangle
and
\lvert\phi_{i}\rangle
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}inv}}B_{i/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}B_{i/2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\|{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}B_{i/2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}B_{i/2}\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle\|_{2}

(by the triangle inequality)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}inv}}B_{i/2}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}})B_{i/2}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle\|_{2}+\|W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}B_{i/2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\lvert\phi_{i-1}\rangle-\lvert\psi_{i-1}\rangle)\|_{2}

(since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
and
B_{i/2}
commute)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}inv}}B_{i/2}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}})\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\norm{\Ocomp\ket{\phi_{i-1}}-\ket{\psi_{i-1}}}_{2}

(by
Lemma

3.6
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(i^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})+O((i-1)^{3}/2^{{\lambda}}).

(by
Lemma

7.8
and the induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(i^{3}/2^{{\lambda}})

7.4
Proof of
Lemma

7.8
: Closeness of the Oracle Queries

To prove
Lemma

7.8
, we start by recalling that

\displaystyle W^{\mathsf{glued}}=

\displaystyle\left(\Pi^{{\mathcal{L}},321}\right)\cdot V_{L}^{(3),\mathsf{mid}}\cdot V_{L}^{(2),\mathsf{mid}}\cdot V_{L}^{(1),\mathsf{mid}}\cdot\left(I-\Pi^{{\mathcal{R}},1}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\Pi^{{\mathcal{L}},32}-\Pi^{{\mathcal{L}},321}\right)\cdot V_{L}^{(3),\mathsf{mid}}\cdot V_{L}^{(2),\mathsf{mid}}\cdot V_{R}^{(1),\mathsf{mid},\dagger}\cdot\left(\Pi^{{\mathcal{R}},1}-\Pi^{{\mathcal{R}},12}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\Pi^{{\mathcal{L}},3}-\Pi^{{\mathcal{L}},32}\right)\cdot V_{L}^{(3),\mathsf{mid}}\cdot V_{R}^{(2),\mathsf{mid},\dagger}\cdot V_{R}^{(1),\mathsf{mid},\dagger}\cdot\left(\Pi^{{\mathcal{R}},12}-\Pi^{{\mathcal{R}},123}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(I-\Pi^{{\mathcal{L}},3}\right)\cdot V_{R}^{(3),\mathsf{mid},\dagger}\cdot V_{R}^{(2),\mathsf{mid},\dagger}\cdot V_{R}^{(1),\mathsf{mid},\dagger}\cdot\left(\Pi^{{\mathcal{R}},123}\right)

And that from
Section

6.5
, we know a nice basis for "good" states in the four subspaces on which we have different operations of
W^{\mathsf{glued}}
. Hence, we start by proving that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}
works in this four subspaces. Formally, we show the following lemmas:

Lemma 7.9
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},1}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

Lemma 7.10
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},2}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

Lemma 7.11
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},3}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

Lemma 7.12
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},4}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

We give proofs of the above lemmas in
Appendix

E
.

We restate
Lemma

7.8
for convenience.

Lemma 7.13

(
Lemma

7.8
, restated)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

•

Forward query:

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

•

Inverse query:

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}inv}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda}),\dagger}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

Proof.

We prove the lemma for forward queries and we get it for inverse queries symmetrically. We want to show

\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}}).

First, notice that
\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},i}=I
. Hence, we get

\displaystyle\gamma=

\displaystyle\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},i}\right)\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{i=1}^{4}\left(\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},i}\right)\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|\left(\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}W^{\mathsf{glued\text{-}fwd}}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{comp}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi^{\mathfrak{l},i}\right)\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(t^{2}/2^{{\lambda}})

Where the fouth line is by triangle inequality, and fifth line is by
Lemmas

7.9
,
7.10
,
7.11
and
7.12
.
∎

8
Stretching Strong Pseudorandom Unitaries

Now we show how to apply our results to get nearly linear depth and to stretch the length of
any
strong pseudorandom unitary, relative to its key size.

To prove that we can stretch the keys in a PRU family, we start by recalling the following result from
[
ABGL25a
]
,

Theorem 8.1
.

For any
f(n)=\omega(\log n)
,
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\{0,1\}^{f(n)}
, define

G^{U}(k_{1}||k_{2}||k_{3}):=(X^{k_{3}}\otimes I_{n-f(n)})U(X^{k_{2}}\otimes I_{n-f(n)})U(X^{k_{1}}\otimes I_{n-f(n)}),

where
U
is an
n
-qubit unitary. Then
\{G_{U}(k_{1}||k_{2}||k_{3})\}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\{0,1\}^{f(n)}}
is a strong PRU in the QHROM.

At a high level, from a single instance of a strong pseudorandom unitary for a key
k
, and
9
random strings of length
O(\log^{2}(n))
, we can create three additional instances of a strong pseudorandom unitaries, that are random even relative to the original instance of the pseudorandom unitary, by applying the construction above thrice. Then we can apply the strong gluing theorem to join these three pseudorandom unitaries into a strong pseudorandom unitary acting on a larger input. Formally, we have the following theorem.

Theorem 8.2

(Stretching a strong PRU)
.

Let
\{\mathsf{PRU}_{\lambda,k}\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{N},k\in\{0,1\}^{\lambda}}
be a strong pseudorandom unitary family with keys of size
\lambda
acting on
t(\lambda)
many qubits. Then there exists a family of strong pseudorandom unitaries
\{\mathsf{StretchPRU}_{\lambda,k\in\{0,1\}^{\lambda+9\log^{2}(\lambda)}}\}
with keys of length
\lambda+9\log^{2}(\lambda)
that acts on
2t(\lambda)-\log^{2}(\lambda)
qubits.

Proof of
Theorem

8.2
.

Let
k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}
be a string of length
9\log^{2}(\lambda)
where each
k_{i}
is length
\log^{2}(\lambda)
. Then consider the following construction of
\mathsf{StretchPRU}
on registers
\mathsf{ABC}
, where
\mathsf{A}
is
t(\lambda)-\log^{2}(\lambda)
qubits,
\mathsf{B}
is
\log^{2}(\lambda)
qubits, and
\mathsf{C}
is
t(\lambda)-\log^{2}(\lambda)
qubits.

\mathsf{StretchPRU}_{k||k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}}=\\
(X^{k_{1}}\mathsf{PRU}_{k}X^{k_{2}}\mathsf{PRU}_{k}X^{k_{3}})_{\mathsf{AB}}(X^{k_{4}}\mathsf{PRU}_{k}X^{k_{5}}\mathsf{PRU}_{k}X^{k_{6}})_{\mathsf{BC}}(X^{k_{7}}\mathsf{PRU}_{k}X^{k_{8}}\mathsf{PRU}_{k}X^{k_{9}})_{\mathsf{AB}}\,.

Let
\mathsf{Stretch}_{k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}}(U)
be the same construction, except that
\mathsf{PRU}_{k}
is replaced with a unitary
U
. Then by the definition of a strong pseudo-random unitary, we have the following for all polynomial-time adversaries
\mathcal{A}
.

\Bigg|\Pr_{k||k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda+9\log^{2}(\lambda)}}\left[\top\leftarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{StretchPRU}_{k||k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}},\mathsf{StretchPRU}_{k||k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}}^{\dagger}}\right]\\
-\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\leftarrow\mu_{t(\lambda)}\\
k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{9\log^{2}(\lambda)}\end{subarray}}\left[\top\leftarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Stretch}_{k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}}(U),\left(\mathsf{Stretch}_{k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}}(U)\right)^{\dagger}}\right]\Bigg|\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)\,.

From
Theorem

8.1
, applied three times, we have the following bound:

\Bigg|\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\leftarrow\mu_{t(\lambda)}\\
k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{9\log^{2}(\lambda)}\end{subarray}}\left[\top\leftarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Stretch}_{k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}}(U),\left(\mathsf{Stretch}_{k_{1}||\ldots||k_{9}}(U)\right)^{\dagger}}\right]\\
-\Pr_{U^{\prime},V,W\leftarrow\mu_{t(\lambda)}}\left[\top\leftarrow\mathcal{A}^{U^{\prime}VW,(U^{\prime}VW)^{\dagger}}\right]\Bigg|\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)\,.

Finally, applying
Theorem

7.1
, we have the following:

\Bigg|\Pr_{U^{\prime},V,W\leftarrow\mu_{t(\lambda)}}\left[\top\leftarrow\mathcal{A}^{U^{\prime}VW,(U^{\prime}VW)^{\dagger}}\right]-\Pr_{O\leftarrow\mu_{2t(\lambda)-\log^{2}(\lambda)}}\left[\top\leftarrow\mathcal{A}^{O,O^{\dagger}}\right]\Bigg|\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)\,.

Applying the triangle inequality, the construction of
\mathsf{StretchPRU}
is indistinguishable from a large Haar random unitary on
2t(\lambda)-\log^{2}(\lambda)
qubits.
∎

Corollary 8.3

(Strong pseudorandom unitaries with small keys)
.

If there exists a family of strong pseudorandom unitaries, then for every constant
c
there exists a strong pseudorandom unitary family such that

1.

The key size is
\lambda+9c\log^{3}(\lambda)
.

2.

The pseudorandom unitary family acts on
\lambda^{c}(t(\lambda)-\log^{2}(\lambda))+\log^{2}(\lambda)
qubits.

Proof of
Corollary

8.3
.

We recursively apply the previous theorem
c\log(\lambda)
many times. Each time, we need
9\log^{2}(\lambda)
additional bits of randomness, and we double (minus
9\log^{2}(\lambda)
) the output length of the strong pseudorandom unitary. Thus, after performing this transformation recursively
n
times, our output length is

2^{n}t(\lambda)-2^{n-1}\cdot 9\log^{2}(\lambda)-2^{n-2}\cdot 9\log^{2}(\lambda)-\ldots-9\log^{2}(\lambda)=2^{n}(t(\lambda)-9\log^{2}(\lambda))\,.

This setting
n=c\log(\lambda)
, we get the desired key length and output length. Note that this requires running the original strong pseudorandom unitary
O(\lambda^{c})
times, which is polynomial in
\lambda
for constant
c
.
∎

Rescaling so that
\lambda\leftarrow\lambda+9c\log^{3}(\lambda)
, we have that there is a family of strong pseudo-random unitaries with keys of length
\lambda
and output size roughly
\lambda^{c}
.

Next, we prove that our strong gluing theorem implies that strong PRUs exist at near linear depth.

Corollary 8.4

(Shortening a super-linear depth PRU)
.

If there exists a family of strong pseudorandom unitaries, then for every constant
c
there exists a family of strong pseudorandom unitaries with depth
O(n^{1+1/c})
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}=\{{\mathcal{G}}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
denote a strong pseudorandom unitary family with
{\mathcal{G}}^{n}
denoting the unitaries with input length
n
. Let
{\mathcal{K}}^{n}
denote the set of keys assoicated with
{\mathcal{G}}^{n}
. By the definition of strong pseudorandom unitaries, the depth of any circuit in
{\mathcal{G}}^{n}
is asymptotically bounded by a polynomial in
n
, the input size. Let
c_{1}
be a constant such that the depth of the family of strong pseudorandom unitaries is asymptotically bounded by
O(n^{c_{1}})
.

Then circuits in
{\mathcal{G}}^{n^{1/(c_{1}\cdot c)}}
are strong PRUs on input length
n^{1/(c_{1}\cdot c)}
qubits, whose depth is bounded by
O(n^{1/c})
. Then for any
t\in\mathsf{poly}(n)
, we sample some
k_{1},\ldots,k_{2t+1}\leftarrow{\mathcal{K}}^{n^{1/(c_{1}\cdot c)}}
. We arrange
U^{k_{1}},\ldots,U^{k_{2t+1}}
in the following circuit:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \ldots\ }

\mathsf{A_{1}}

U^{k_{1}}

U^{k_{1}}

U^{k_{2t+1}}

U^{k_{2t+1}}

\mathsf{A_{2}}

U^{k_{2}}

U^{k_{2}}

U^{k_{2t}}

U^{k_{2t}}

\mathsf{A_{3}}

\vdots

\mathsf{A_{t}}

U^{k_{t}}

U^{k_{t}}

U^{k_{t+2}}

U^{k_{t+2}}

\mathsf{A_{t+1}}

U^{k_{t+1}}

U^{k_{t+1}}

\mathsf{A_{t+2}}

In the above,
|A_{i}|=n^{1/(c_{1}\cdot c)}/2
. Notice that the above circuit is on input size
(t+2)\cdot n^{1/(c_{1}\cdot c)}/2
that has depth
(2t+1)\cdot O(n^{1/c})
. Let
t=O(n)
, we have a family of circuits on input length
O(n)
that has depth
O(n^{1+1/c})
.

We prove sampling
k_{1},\ldots,k_{2t+1}\leftarrow{\mathcal{K}}^{n^{1/(c_{1}\cdot c)}}
and arranging as above gives us a family of strong PRUs with input length
O(n)
that has depth
O(n^{1+1/c})
. To prove that the above circuit is a PRU, we start by applying the strong gluing theorem on the middle three unitaries (i.e.
U^{k_{t}}U^{k_{t+1}}U^{k_{t+2}}
) and replacing it with a larger Haar unitary (say
V_{1}
). Next, we apply the strong gluing theorem on the new middle three unitaries (i.e.
U^{k_{t-1}}V_{1}U^{k_{t+3}}
) and replacing it with a larger Haar unitary (say
V_{2}
). Repeating this process a total of
t
times gives us a single large Haar unitary.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of
Lemma

4.3

We start by proving that
V_{L}^{f_{L}}
is close to
V_{L}
and
V_{R}^{f_{R}}
is close to
V_{R}
.

Lemma A.1
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|(V_{L}-V_{L}^{f_{L}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{2\cdot(t+1)\cdot\delta}\quad\text{and}\quad\|(V_{R}-V_{R}^{f_{R}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{2\cdot(t+1)\cdot\delta}.

Proof.

Consider an arbitrary (normalized) state in the support of
\Pi_{\leq t}

\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{ABST}}=\sum_{x,z,L,R}\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}},

where
\alpha_{x,z,L,R}=0
whenever
|L\cup R|>t
. Then

V_{L}\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{ABST}}=\sum_{x,z,L,R}\frac{\alpha_{x,z,L,R}}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}\sum_{y\notin\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}},

and

V_{L}^{f_{L}}\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{ABST}}=\sum_{x,z,L,R}\frac{\alpha_{x,z,L,R}}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}}\sum_{y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}.

Subtracting,

\displaystyle(V_{L}

\displaystyle-V_{L}^{f_{L}})\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{ABST}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,z,L,R}\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y:y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}}\right)}_{\lvert v\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{x,z,L,R}\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y:y\not\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}\right)}_{\lvert w_{1}\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{x,z,L,R}\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y:y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}}\right)}_{\lvert w_{2}\rangle}.

Note that
\lvert w_{1}\rangle
,
\lvert w_{2}\rangle
and
\lvert v\rangle
are orthogonal. Thus,

\norm{(V_L - V_L^{f_L})\ket{\psi}_{\reg{ABST}}}^{2}_{2}=\langle v|v\rangle+\langle w_{1}|w_{1}\rangle+\langle w_{2}|w_{2}\rangle

Bounding
\langle v|v\rangle
.
Similar to
[
MH24
]
, by changing the order of summation, we can rewrite
\lvert v\rangle
as

\displaystyle\lvert v\rangle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle\lvert z\rangle\lvert L^{\prime}\rangle\lvert R\rangle\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}}\right)\right),

and thus

\displaystyle\langle v|v\rangle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}|\alpha_{x,z,L,R}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}}\right)^{2}\right),

where the last inequality is by Cauchy-Schwarz. We can bound the summand by writing

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}-\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|(2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|)}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\left|2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|-|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|\right|}}{\sqrt{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|(2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|)}}\right)^{2}

(since
\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}\leq\sqrt{a-b}
when
a\geq b\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)\cdot\left|2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|-|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|\right|}{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|(2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|)}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\cdot\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)}{(2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|)}

where the last inequality uses the fact that for any fixed
L^{\prime}
, there are at most
\absolutevalue{L}+1
choices of
(x,L)
that can satisfy
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\langle v|v\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\delta\cdot\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)}{(2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|)}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\alpha_{x,z,L,R}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta\cdot\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)}{(2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|)}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,z,L,R\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\alpha_{x,z,L,R}}^{2}\cdot\left(\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}^{2n+{\lambda}}}\mathds{1}(y\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R))\mathds{1}(y\notin\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1}))\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta\cdot(\absolutevalue{L}+1)\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,z,L,R\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\alpha_{x,z,L,R}}^{2}=\delta\cdot(\absolutevalue{L}+1).

Bounding
\langle w_{1}|w_{1}\rangle
.
We know that

\displaystyle\lvert w_{1}\rangle=

\displaystyle\sum_{x,z,L,R}\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y:y\not\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y,z,(L^{\prime},R)}\lvert y\rangle\lvert z\rangle\lvert L^{\prime}\rangle\lvert R\rangle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{x,z,L,R}}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}\right)

Then

\displaystyle\langle w_{1}|w_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{y,z,(L^{\prime},R)}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{x,z,L,R}}{\sqrt{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}}\right|^{2}\leq\sum_{y,z,(L^{\prime},R)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\right|^{2}}{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,z,L,R}\frac{\left|\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\right|^{2}}{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y:y\not\in f_{L}(x,z,L,R)\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}1\right)\leq\sum_{x,z,L,R}\frac{(2^{n}-|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|)\left|\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\right|^{2}}{2^{n}-|\imaginary(L\cup R^{-1})|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{\delta+1}\sum_{x,z,L,R}\left|\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\right|^{2}=\frac{\delta}{\delta+1}\leq\delta

Similarly, we also have

\langle w_{2}|w_{2}\rangle\leq\sum_{x,z,L,R}\frac{t\left|\alpha_{x,z,L,R}\right|^{2}}{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}\leq t\cdot\delta.

Hence, it holds that

\|(V_{L}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}_{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{2\cdot(t+1)\cdot\delta}.

By a symmetric argument, we have

\|(V_{R}-W^{\mathfrak{m}({\lambda})}_{R})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{2\cdot(t+1)\cdot\delta}.\qed

Then by applying triangle inequality multiple times, we have:

Lemma A.2
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|(V-V^{f_{L},f_{R}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 8\sqrt{2\cdot(t+1)\cdot\delta}.

Finally, by bounding each query distance by
\delta
, we get the following:

Lemma A.3

(Generalized Path-Recording (restated))
.

Let
f_{L}
and
f_{R}
be functions such that for all
x,z,L,R
,
|L|+|R|\leq t
,

\displaystyle f_{L}(x,z,L,R)

\displaystyle\subseteq[N]\setminus\imaginary(L)

\displaystyle f_{R}(x,z,L,R)

\displaystyle\subseteq[N]\setminus\imaginary(R)

\displaystyle\frac{\left|N-|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|-t\right|}{|f_{L}(x,z,L,R)|}

\displaystyle\leq\delta

\displaystyle\frac{\left|N-|f_{R}(x,z,L,R)|-t\right|}{|f_{R}(x,z,L,R)|}

\displaystyle\leq\delta,

For any
2t
-query algorithm
{\cal A}=\left(A_{1},B_{1},\ldots,A_{t},B_{t}\right)
,

\mathsf{TD}\quantity( \Tr_{\reg{ST}} \left( \ketbra{\Adversary_t^{V^{f_L,f_R},V^{f_L,f_R,\dagger}}}{\Adversary_t^{V^{f_L,f_R},V^{f_L,f_R,\dagger}}}\right), \Tr_{\reg{ST}} \left( \ketbra{\Adversary_t^{V,V^{\dagger}}}{\Adversary_t^{V,V^{\dagger}}}\right) )\leq 16t\sqrt{2\cdot(t+1)\cdot\delta},

where,

\displaystyle\lvert{\cal A}_{t}^{V^{f_{L},f_{R}},V^{f_{L},f_{R},\dagger}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(V^{f_{L},f_{R},\dagger}B_{i}V^{f_{L},f_{R}}A_{i}\right)\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\lvert{\cal A}_{t}^{V,V^{\dagger}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(V^{\dagger}B_{i}VA_{i}\right)\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}

Appendix B
Glued Path Recording

We want to show that querying
V^{3}V^{2}V^{1}
is close to querying
V^{\mathsf{glued}}
. We start by formalising the intuition we had about monogamy of entanglement by proving the following lemma:

Lemma B.1
.

Let
V^{1}_{L}
be such that it acts on
\mathsf{ABS_{1}T_{1}}
and
V_{R}^{2}
be such that it acts on
\mathsf{BCS_{2}T_{2}}
, then

\|V_{L}^{1,\dagger}V_{R}^{2}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{2^{{\lambda}}}\right).

Proof.

Let

\lvert\psi\rangle=\sum_{x,L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}}\alpha_{xL_{1}L_{2}R_{1}R_{2}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{ABC}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}.

Then let
\lvert\chi\rangle=V_{L}^{1,\dagger}V_{R}^{2}\lvert\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\lvert\chi\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{y,L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}}\alpha_{yL_{1}L_{2}R_{1}R_{2}}V_{L}^{1,\dagger}V_{R}^{2}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{ABC}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n+{\lambda}}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
x\not\in\operatorname{Dom}(L\cup R^{-1})\end{subarray}}\alpha_{yL_{1}L_{2}R_{1}R_{2}}V_{L}^{1,\dagger}\lvert y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{BC}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\cup\{(y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})},x)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n+{\lambda}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
x\not\in\operatorname{Dom}(L_{2}\cup R_{2}^{-1})\\
z:(z,y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}||x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})})\in L_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{yL_{1}L_{2}R_{1}R_{2}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{AB}}\lvert x^{\mathfrak{r}(n)}\rangle_{\mathsf{C}}\lvert L_{1}\setminus\{(z,y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}||x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})})\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\cup\{(y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})},x)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n+{\lambda}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
x\not\in\operatorname{Dom}(L_{2}\cup R_{2})\\
z:(z,y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}||x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})})\in L_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{yL_{1}L_{2}R_{1}R_{2}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{AB}}\lvert x^{\mathfrak{r}(n)}\rangle_{\mathsf{C}}\lvert L_{1}\setminus\{(z,y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}||x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})})\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\cup\{(y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})},x)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n+{\lambda}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y,z,L^{\prime}_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R^{\prime}_{2}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{yL^{\prime}_{1}\cup\{(z,y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}||x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})})\}L_{2}R_{1}R^{\prime}_{2}\setminus\{(y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})},x)\}}\lvert z\rangle_{\mathsf{AB}}\lvert x^{\mathfrak{r}(n)}\rangle_{\mathsf{C}}\lvert L^{\prime}_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R^{\prime}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

Then
\|\lvert\chi\rangle\|^{2}_{2}=\langle\chi|\chi\rangle
,

\displaystyle\|\lvert\chi\rangle\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2(n+{\lambda})}}\sum_{z,x^{\mathfrak{r}(n)},L^{\prime}_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\left|\sum_{y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)},y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})},x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})}}\alpha_{yL^{\prime}_{1}\cup\{(z,y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}||x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})})\}L_{2}R_{1}R^{\prime}_{2}\setminus\{(x,y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})})\}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t^{2}2^{2n}}{2^{2(n+{\lambda})}}\sum_{z,x^{\mathfrak{r}(n)},L^{\prime}_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\sum_{y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)},y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})},x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})}}\left|\alpha_{yL^{\prime}_{1}\cup\{(z,y^{\mathfrak{l}(n)}||x^{\mathfrak{l}({\lambda})})\}L_{2}R_{1}R^{\prime}_{2}\setminus\{(x,y^{\mathfrak{r}(n+{\lambda})})\}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t^{4}}{2^{2{\lambda}}}

Hence

\|V_{L}^{1,\dagger}V_{R}^{2}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{t^{2}}{2^{{\lambda}}}.

∎

Lemma B.2
.

Let
V^{\mathsf{glued}}
be defined as before, then

\|(V^{\mathsf{glued}}-V^{3}V^{2}V^{1})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{2^{{\lambda}}}\right).

The above lemma gives us the following:

Lemma B.3

(
Lemma

5.1
restated)
.

For any
2t
-query algorithm
{\cal A}=\left(A_{1},B_{1},\ldots,A_{t},B_{t}\right)
,

\norm{\ket{\Adversary^{V^{\glued}, V^{\glued,\dagger}}}_{\reg{ABCD\overline{ST}}}
- \ket{\Adversary^{V^{3}V^{2}V^{1}, (V^{3}V^{2}V^{1})^{\dagger}}}_{\reg{ABCD\overline{ST}}}}_{2}=O\quantity(\frac{t^3}{2^{\secp}}).

where
\mathsf{\overline{ST}}=\mathsf{S_{1}S_{2}S_{3}T_{1}T_{2}T_{3}}
,
|\mathsf{ABC}|=2n+{\lambda}
,

\lvert{\cal A}_{t}^{V^{\mathsf{glued}},V^{\mathsf{glued},\dagger}}\rangle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(V^{\mathsf{glued},\dagger}B_{i}V^{\mathsf{glued}}A_{i}\right)\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{ABC}}\lvert 0\rangle_{\mathsf{D}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S_{1}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T_{1}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S_{2}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T_{2}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{S_{3}}}\lvert\varnothing\rangle_{\mathsf{T_{3}}}

and
\lvert{\cal A}^{V^{3}V^{2}V^{1},(V^{3}V^{2}V^{1})^{\dagger}}\rangle_{\mathsf{ABCD\overline{ST}}}
is defined similarly.

Appendix C
Proofs of
Section

Proof of

.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n}(2^{\lambda}-a+1)\sqrt{2^{an}\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{b}(2^{\lambda}-i+1)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{(a-1)n}\\
R^{\prime}\in\left(\{0,1\}^{{\lambda}}\right)^{b-|\overrightarrow{x}|}_{\mathrm{dist}}\\
z\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\

y^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}(2^{\lambda}-a+1)}}\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}(2^{\lambda}-a+1)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\

Finally, to understand

, we expanding the projector

:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

Hence,

\Pi^{\mathsf{Good}}\Pi^{{\mathcal{R}},1}=\sum_{\overline{S^{\prime}},X,\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{y},w_{1}}\lvert\chi^{\mathfrak{l},1}_{\overline{S^{\prime}},X,\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{y},w_{1}}\rangle\!\langle\chi^{\mathfrak{l},1}_{\overline{S^{\prime}},X,\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{y},w_{1}}\rvert

∎

Proof of
Lemma

6.11
.

y_{0},w,y_{1},\overline{S}

and

\displaystyle\lvert\psi_{y_{0},w,y_{1},\overline{S}}\rangle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\times\lvert\mathbb{G}\left(\overline{S^{\prime}},R^{\prime},Z^{\prime}\right)\cup\{(X{\mathcal{R}},\overrightarrow{x},y_{0}||\overrightarrow{y}||y_{1},w_{1},w,\overrightarrow{r},z)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{\overline{ST}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{2n}(2^{\lambda}-a+1)\sqrt{2^{an}\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{b}(2^{\lambda}-i+1)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{(a-1)n}\\

z\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\

y_{0}^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\

\displaystyle\times\lvert\mathbb{G}\left(\overline{S^{\prime}},R^{\prime},Z^{\prime}\right)\cup\{(X{\mathcal{R}},\overrightarrow{x},y^{\prime}_{0}||\overrightarrow{y}||y_{1}^{\prime},w_{1},w^{\prime},\overrightarrow{r},z)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{\overline{ST}}}

y_{1}^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
Z\in\{0,1\}^{an}\\
